\begin{nota}[Relative annular ideal]\label{relative:annular:ideal}
Put $B=\ggk\mgRV^{\db}[*]$ and retain the scalar ideal
$I=(\bm P_\Gamma)$ of Notation~\ref{pgammabis}, with exactly its
original RV-liftable radius indexing. For
$\bm U=(U,f,\omega)\in\mRV^{\db}[k]$, $k>0$, suppose that
$\pr_{\widetilde j}\rest f(U)$ is finite-to-one. Let
$\tau:U\to\RV$ be definable with
$0\leq\gamma(u):=\vrv(\tau(u))\leq\Delta$ for one finite named
$\Delta$. Define the relative annular object by the separately tagged
union
\[
 U^{\tau,j}=\{(u,s):0<\vrv(s)\leq\gamma(u)\}
 \ \uplus\ \{(u,\tau(u)):u\in U\}.
\]
Its structural coordinates are $sf_j(u)$ in position $j$ and $f_i(u)$
in every other position; its volume form is $\omega(u)$.
Write $J_{\mathrm{var}}$ for the ideal of $B$ generated by
$[\bm U^{\tau,j}]-[\bm U]$ for all these data. The full aperture graph
is retained, including when its image is infinite. Constant apertures
give $I\subseteq J_{\mathrm{var}}$, but no equality is asserted.
\end{nota}

\begin{lem}[Compatibility with the relative annular ideal]\label{relative:annular:compatibility}
The volume-forgetting map $F:B\to\ggk\RV[*]$ kills
$J_{\mathrm{var}}$. Hence the bounded retractions of this section
factor through $B/J_{\mathrm{var}}$, with their original restrictions
to the residue rings. The ideal is homogeneous and meets the entire
degree-zero subring trivially. Its image in the full measured RV ring
lies in $(\bm P)$.
\end{lem}
\begin{proof}
Retain the actual graph of $\tau$; projection onto $U$ is a definable
isomorphism and preserves the required bounds. Put
\[
 \bm W=(U,f_{\widetilde j},\omega+\vrv(f_j)),\qquad
 \bm W_\gamma=(U,f_{\widetilde j},\omega+\vrv(f_j)+\gamma).
\]
The inactive structural map is finite-to-one, so these are bounded
objects of degree $k-1$. Equality of total weights identifies the
source with $\bm W$ times the structural unit point, and the endpoint
with $\bm W_\gamma$ times that point. The shell has structural map
$(s,f_{\widetilde j})$ and form $\omega+\vrv(f_j)$.

After forgetting volume, the full positive-ray bundle
$C_+=\{(u,s):\vrv(s)>0\}$ is isomorphic to its subobject
$C_{\mathrm{deep}}=\{(u,z):\vrv(z)>\gamma(u)\}$ by
\[
 (u,s)\longmapsto(u,\tau(u)s),
\]
with inverse division by $\tau(u)$. Its complement is the whole
relative shell. Scissors therefore give zero for the shell in the
full ordinary RV ring. The endpoint graph is ordinarily isomorphic
to the source. Thus $F$ kills every generator, and hence the ideal,
before any Euler evaluation. This includes depth zero.

Every generator has positive homogeneous degree, so the degree-zero
projection kills the ideal and proves injectivity on degree zero.
In the measured ring the same shear identifies the positive ray
$C_+(\bm W_\gamma)$ with the deep subray of $C_+(\bm W)$:
the structural shift $\gamma$ equals the shift in the base form.
With $\bm P=[C_+]-[1]$, scissors give the exact identity
\[
 \operatorname{image}([\bm U^{\tau,j}]-[\bm U])
   =([\bm W_\gamma]-[\bm W])\bm P.
\]
This proves the measured ideal containment. Composing ordinary
forgetting with the already constructed Euler maps proves the stated
retraction factorization and leaves their residue restrictions unchanged.
\end{proof}

The pure RV tensor presentation and its intrinsic coefficient-translation
kernel are unchanged. If $q:T\twoheadrightarrow B$ is that presentation,
the relative annular quotient is $T/q^{-1}(J_{\mathrm{var}})$, and
$B/I\to B/J_{\mathrm{var}}$ is the canonical further quotient.
No factorwise scalar-radius presentation of this new quotient is used.

\section{Interlude: RV decomposition and Fubini theorems}\label{csndag}

We continue to work in the original language $\lan{}{RV}{}$, over the
standing parameter base $\mathbb S$. In particular, no cross-section is
added to the language. We use the decomposition below and the
retraction $\mathbb E_b$ of Section~4 to prove the Fubini statements.

For an integer matrix $M=(m_{ab})\in\GL_n(\Z)$, write
\[
 h_M:(\RV^\times)^n\longrightarrow(\RV^\times)^n,\qquad
 (h_M(u))_a=\prod_b u_b^{m_{ab}}.
\]
Thus $h_M^{-1}=h_{M^{-1}}$ and
$\vrv(h_M(u))=M\vrv(u)$. For $I\subseteq\Gamma^\ell$, the notation
$I^\sharp$ in the following lemma means its full inverse image in
$(\RV^\times)^\ell$ under $\vrv$. The singleton zero/infinity element
of $\RV_\infty$ is treated separately by fixing its coordinate positions.

\setcounter{thm}{11}
\begin{lem}[Monomial decomposition]\label{coh:decom}
Let $U\subseteq(\RV^\times)^n$ be definable. There is a finite definable
partition $(D_i)_i$ of $\vrv(U)$ such that, writing
$U_i=U\cap\vrv^{-1}(D_i)$, there are $M_i\in\GL_n(\Z)$,
$0\leq m_i\leq n$, a nonempty definable set
$V_i\subseteq(\RV^\times)^{m_i}$, and a nonempty definable set
$I_i\subseteq\Gamma^{n-m_i}$ with
\[
 \vrv(V_i)=\{\beta_i\},\qquad
 h_{M_i}(U_i)=V_i\times I_i^\sharp .
\]
All sets and maps are definable over the original base $\mathbb S$.
In particular, the partition of $U$ is induced by a partition of
its value support.
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
We give the normal-form step as well as the integer reduction.

\emph{Finite angular data.}
For $e\in\Z^n$, put $u^e=\prod_j u_j^{e_j}$.
The guarded RV normal form obtained from quantifier elimination has
the following consequence. There is a finite definable partition
$(P_a)_a$ of $\Gamma^n$ and, for each $a$, a finite set
$E_a\subseteq\Z^n$ such that, for $X_a=U\cap\vrv^{-1}(P_a)$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item for every $e\in E_a$, the set
$\{\vrv(u^e):u\in X_a\}$ is finite;
\item if $u\in X_a$, $\vrv(u)=\vrv(u')$, and
$u^e=(u')^e$ for every $e\in E_a$, then $u'\in X_a$.
\end{enumerate}
Here is the finite syntactic argument for this consequence.
In the guarded normal form, a monomial is $c u^e$ and a residue
polynomial is a finite sum of such monomials on their residue
domains. Partition the value space according to the residue-domain
status of every summand and the equality of the values of the two
outer monomials in every equality. Retain all nonzero and domain
guards and both signs of every literal; this partitions the entire
Boolean formula, rather than just covering it by true clauses.
A nonzero summand contributing to a residue polynomial forces
\[
 e\cdot\vrv(u)=-\vrv(c).
\]
Include its exponent $e$ among the active characters. A zero
coefficient contributes nothing and needs no character.
In an equality whose two nonzero outer coefficients have the same
value, include the difference of their exponent vectors: its value
is fixed by the quotient of those coefficients. If their values are
different, the equality is possible precisely when both residue
factors vanish. Thus every remaining angular test depends only on
these finitely many characters. Order tests depend only on the
coordinate values. This proves (1) and (2), including negated
literals.

The value partition is defined over $\mathbb S$. To check this if RV
representatives of named value-group parameters are used while
obtaining the normal form, the values of nonzero coefficients
descend by the original-base Gamma parameter-control theorem:
the value of a nonzero coefficient definable from these names
lies in $\dcl^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbb S)\cap\Gamma$.
The partition uses only those coefficient values. No chosen
RV representative is retained as a new parameter.

\emph{Integer coordinates.}
Fix $a$ and let $N$ have rows the characters in $E_a$.
Column Hermite reduction supplies $A\in\GL_n(\Z)$ with
\[
 NA=(C\ \ 0),\qquad \operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Q}C=m,
\]
where $C$ has $m$ columns. This is an integer change of basis:
column additions, interchanges and sign changes suffice.
Set $z=h_{A^{-1}}(u)=(v,w)$ with $v\in(\RV^\times)^m$.
By (1), $C\vrv(v)$ has finite image on $h_{A^{-1}}(X_a)$.
The map $\beta\mapsto C\beta$ on $\Gamma^m$ is injective, since
$C$ has full column rank and the ordered group $\Gamma$ is
torsion-free. Consequently $\vrv(v)$ has finite image as well.
These finitely many tuples are individually $\mathbb S$-definable:
a finite definable subset of the ordered set $\Gamma^m$ can be
enumerated lexicographically. Refine $P_a$ by these value tuples
and fix one, denoted by $\beta$.

On this piece, membership of $(v,w)$ depends only on $v$ and
$\vrv(w)$. Indeed, changing $w$ within its value fiber keeps all
coordinate values of $u=h_A(v,w)$ unchanged, while $NA=(C\ 0)$
keeps every active character unchanged. Property (2) applies in
both directions. Therefore this piece is the full inverse image
of a definable relation
\[
 T\subseteq\beta^\sharp\times\Gamma^{n-m}
\]
under $(v,w)\mapsto(v,\vrv(w))$.

\emph{Separate the residue and value data over the original base.}
Temporarily choose a point of $\beta^\sharp$ to identify this
fiber with a residue-field torus. Residue-field/Gamma
orthogonality then implies that only finitely many distinct sets
\[
 T_\eta=\{v\in\beta^\sharp:(v,\eta)\in T\},
 \qquad \eta\in\Gamma^{n-m},
\]
occur. This finiteness does not retain the chosen point as a
parameter.

We spell out descent to $\mathbb S$. Put
$\Gamma_0=\dcl^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbb S)\cap\Gamma$.
The equivalence relation
\[
 \eta\sim\eta' \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \forall v\in\beta^\sharp\
 \big((v,\eta)\in T\ \longleftrightarrow\ (v,\eta')\in T\big)
\]
is $\mathbb S$-definable. By the original-base Gamma
parameter-control consequence of quantifier elimination, it is
definable in the pure ordered divisible group over $\Gamma_0$.
The standing nontriviality assumption makes $\Gamma_0$ a
nontrivial divisible ordered subgroup, so
$\Gamma_0\preceq\Gamma$. If there are $q$ equivalence classes,
the assertion that $q$ pairwise inequivalent representatives
exist is a first-order assertion over $\Gamma_0$. Hence we may
take representatives $\eta_1,\ldots,\eta_q\in\Gamma_0^{n-m}$.
Set
\[
 I_j=\{\eta:\eta\sim\eta_j\},\qquad V_j=T_{\eta_j}.
\]
These sets are definable over $\mathbb S$, the $I_j$ form a
partition, and
$T\cap(\beta^\sharp\times I_j)=V_j\times I_j$.
Discard the empty residue fibers. Each remaining piece has
full RV preimage $V_j\times I_j^\sharp$ and
$\vrv(V_j)=\{\beta\}$.

The corresponding original value piece is
$D_j=A(\{\beta\}\times I_j)$. It lies in the chosen $P_a$:
choose any $v\in V_j$ and lift each $\eta\in I_j$ to see this.
The $D_j$ partition the value support of the fixed-beta piece
of $X_a$.
Doing this for each $a$ proves the assertion with
$M_i=A^{-1}$. The cases $m=0$, $m=n$ and $U=\varnothing$
are covered by the usual empty-tuple conventions.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Tropical motivic Fubini theorem}\label{jp:fubini}
In this subsection we work in the field $\widetilde{\C}$ of complex
Puiseux series, considered as an elementary substructure of
$\mathbb U$. Put $F=\C((t))$ and take the parameter base to be
$\mathbb S=F$. Normalize the valuation by $v(t)=1$, so that the
value group of $\widetilde{\C}$ is identified with $\Q$.
All definability and all categories are interpreted in the original
language $\lan{}{RV}{}$ over $\mathbb S$. In particular, each
$a/N\in\Q$ is definable over $\mathbb S$ as the unique solution of
$N\gamma=a\,v(t)$.

\begin{rem}[The finite Kummer actions]\label{puiseux:kummer:action}
There are natural identifications
\[
 \gal(\widetilde{\C}/F)\ \cong\ \widehat{\mu}
 \ \cong\ \aut\bigl(\RV(\widetilde{\C})/\RV(F)\bigr),
 \qquad
 \widehat{\mu}=\varprojlim_N\mu_N,
\]
where the transition map $\mu_M\to\mu_N$ for $N\mid M$
is $\zeta\mapsto\zeta^{M/N}$, and the last automorphism group
uses the induced original-language structure on $\RV$.

For the proof, fix $N\geq1$, choose any root $\tau_N$ of
$X^N-t$ in $\widetilde{\C}$, and put
$F_N=\C((\tau_N))$. Grouping exponents modulo $N$ writes each
element of $F_N$ uniquely as
\[
 \sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\tau_N^j f_j(t),\qquad f_j(t)\in F.
\]
Uniqueness also follows from the pairwise distinct valuations
modulo $\Z$ of the nonzero summands. Thus $[F_N:F]=N$.
Its $F$-automorphisms are precisely
$\tau_N\mapsto\zeta\tau_N$ for $\zeta\in\mu_N$.
Replacing $\tau_N$ by another root multiplies it by an element
of $\mu_N\subseteq F$, so both $F_N$ and the ratio
$\sigma(\tau_N)/\tau_N$ are independent of that choice.
For $N\mid M$ these ratios satisfy
\[
 \frac{\sigma(\tau_N)}{\tau_N}
 =
 \left(\frac{\sigma(\tau_M)}{\tau_M}\right)^{M/N},
\]
since $\tau_M^{M/N}/\tau_N\in\mu_N\subseteq F$.
The fields $F_N$ have union $\widetilde{\C}$.
Their finite automorphisms therefore give exactly the compatible
systems $(\zeta_N)_N\in\widehat{\mu}$, proving the first
identification. Each such automorphism preserves the valuation:
in the displayed expansion it multiplies the $j$th summand by
$\zeta_N^j$, preserving its valuation and the unique least
valuation of a nonzero sum.

Write $r_N=\rv(\tau_N)$ and identify $\C^\times$ with the
value-zero subgroup of $\RV(\widetilde{\C})$.
For any $u$ of value $a/N$ there is a unique $c\in\C^\times$
such that $u=c\,r_N^a$. The induced action is
\[
 \sigma_{(\zeta_N)}(c\,r_N^a)
   =c\,\zeta_N^a r_N^a .
\]
In particular, a nonidentity system moves $r_N$ for some $N$.
The action on $\RV$ is faithful.

Finally, $\RV(F)$ is the subgroup generated by
$\C^\times$ and $\rv(t)$, by taking the leading term of a
Laurent series. An automorphism of the induced RV structure
fixing $\RV(F)$ must send $r_N$ to
$\zeta_Nr_N$ for some $\zeta_N\in\mu_N$: these are precisely
the $N$ roots of $\rv(t)$ in $\RV$.
The same relations as above force compatibility of the
$\zeta_N$. The displayed action then determines the
automorphism on every RV element. It agrees with the unique
valued-field automorphism constructed from that system.
This proves the second identification.
The parameter base for all these definability assertions is
still $\mathbb S=F$.
\end{rem}

\begin{nota}[Equivariant residue realization]\label{bipo:gdv}
Write $\ggk^{\widehat\mu,\mathrm{raw}}\var_{\C}$ for the ring with good finite
$\widehat\mu$-actions and the equivariant scissor relations.
Here \emph{good} means that the action factors through some $\mu_N$
and every orbit is contained in an affine open subset.
Write $\gmv$ for its quotient by the ideal generated by
\[
 [X\times(\A^r,\rho)]-[X\times\A^r],
\]
for every good equivariant $X$ and every finite linear action
$\rho$; the action on the second affine factor on the right is
trivial. Goodness and this additional relation are separate
conditions.

There is a canonical isomorphism
\[
 \Theta:\sggk\RES\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\gmv .
\]
We construct it at a finite Kummer level and prove both
directions of the comparison below.
\end{nota}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Finite-level coordinates and the raw comparison.}
For $q\in\Q$, let $L_q=q^\sharp\sqcup\{0_q\}$ be the
one-dimensional residue vector space whose nonzero part is
$q^\sharp=\vrv^{-1}(q)$. The notation $0_q$ records its zero
in that fixed vector space, encoded using the existing RV
zero/infinity element. It introduces no second RV zero.

For $U\in\RES$, split its finite value support and its zero
coordinate positions. Each piece can be regarded as a definable
subset of $\prod_{j=1}^r L_{a_j/N}$ for a common $N>0$ and
integers $a_j$. Choose one root $\tau_N^N=t$ and put
$r_N=\rv(\tau_N)$. The coordinate map
\[
 g_{r_N}(u_1,\ldots,u_r)
   =(u_1/r_N^{a_1},\ldots,u_r/r_N^{a_r})
\]
takes this product to $\C^r$, with $0_q$ mapped to $0$.
Residue-field purity makes $X_U=g_{r_N}(U)$ constructible.
The action of $\mu_N$ on its coordinates is
\[
 (x_j)_j\longmapsto(\zeta^{a_j}x_j)_j .
\]
The set $X_U$ is invariant because $U$ is defined over $F$.
Replacing $r_N$ by $\eta r_N$ multiplies the $j$th coordinate
by $\eta^{-a_j}$, an equivariant algebraic isomorphism.
Enlarging $N$ only inflates the action along
$\mu_M\to\mu_N$ and changes coordinates by such constants.
Thus the resulting equivariant constructible class is independent
of both choices. Products use a common Kummer level, so the
assignment respects products as well as scissors.

We now prove that every equivariant constructible set and
bijection is obtained in this way. A good finite action admits a
finite invariant affine stratification: intersect the translates
of an affine neighborhood of each orbit, take a finite such
cover, and successively remove the preceding opens. These
intersections are affine because the varieties are separated.
For an affine $\mu_N$-variety $X$, take algebra generators of
$\C[X]$, span their finite orbits, and diagonalize that finite
dimensional cyclic representation. Its eigenvectors still
generate the algebra. They give an equivariant closed embedding
\[
 X\hookrightarrow\A^r,\qquad
 \zeta\cdot(x_j)_j=(\zeta^{a_j}x_j)_j
\]
for suitable integers $a_j$.

Let
\[
 T_N=\{r\in\RV:r^N=\rv(t)\}.
\]
For any invariant constructible
$X\subseteq\A^r$ with these coordinate weights, define
\[
 U_X=\left\{u\in\prod_jL_{a_j/N}:
       \exists r\in T_N\quad g_r(u)\in X\right\}.
\]
This is an original-language $F$-definable RES set.
Every two elements of $T_N$ differ by an element of $\mu_N$.
Invariance of $X$ therefore implies
$g_r(U_X)=X$ for every $r\in T_N$.
This proves surjectivity without adding a root to the base.

The same construction applies to the graph of an equivariant
constructible bijection $f:X\to Y$, using one common level:
\[
 G_f=\{(u,v):\exists r\in T_N,\
              (g_r(u),g_r(v))\in\operatorname{Graph}(f)\}.
\]
Equivariance makes the condition independent of $r$, so $G_f$
is the graph of an $F$-definable bijection.
Conversely, framing the graph of an $F$-definable RES bijection
gives an equivariant constructible bijection.
In characteristic zero such a bijection is piecewise an
algebraic isomorphism: its generic graph over each irreducible
piece has degree one, hence a rational inverse on a dense open;
remove those opens and induct on dimension. Intersecting the
finitely many translates makes the partition invariant.
Consequently these graph constructions also respect the usual
equivariant scissor presentation. They show independence of the
chosen affine embeddings and stratifications and give inverse
maps on classes. We have proved an isomorphism
\[
 \Theta_{\mathrm{raw}}:\ggk\RES\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\ggk^{\widehat\mu,\mathrm{raw}}\var_{\C} .
\]

\emph{The quotient.}
For $q=a/N$, the space $L_q$ is sent to the affine line with
character $\rho_a(\zeta)x=\zeta^a x$. Its zero is a fixed point.
Hence
\[
 \Theta_{\mathrm{raw}}
 \bigl([q^\sharp]-[\G_m]\bigr)
 =
 [\A^1,\rho_a]-[\A^1,\mathrm{triv}].
\]
Every finite character of $\widehat\mu$ has this form.
Every finite linear cyclic action is a sum of characters, so its
class is a product of these line classes. By expanding the
difference of two such products, all the linear-action relations,
also after multiplication by an arbitrary $[X]$, are generated
by the displayed line differences. The two quotient ideals
therefore correspond exactly. This proves the asserted
isomorphism $\Theta$.

For later computations, let
$h_{M_i}(U_i)=V_i\times I_i^\sharp$ be a monomial decomposition
as in Lemma~5.12, with $\ell_i$ free RV coordinates.
The original-language construction of $\bb E_b$ in Section~4 gives
\begin{equation}\label{twist:C}
 (\Theta\circ\bb E_b)([\bm U])
   =\sum_i\chi_b(I_i)\,\Theta([V_i])[\G_m]^{\ell_i}.
\end{equation}
The finite-to-one label and its degree are retained as in the
product construction of Section~3; the displayed target forgets
that grading. The formula is an evaluation of the already defined
retraction, so it is independent of the decomposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{nota}\label{mov:vol}
The motivic volume operator is
\[
 \Vol=\Theta\circ\bb E_b\circ\int:
 \ggk\VF_*\longrightarrow\gmv .
\]
Here $\bb E_b$ is applied after the quotient by $(\bm P-1)$,
on which it is already defined by Section~4.
\end{nota}

Let $\mathcal A\subseteq\Gamma^n\times\VF^m\times\RV_\infty^s$
be an $F$-definable family whose fibers $A_\gamma$ are objects
of $\VF_*$. All families below are first interpreted in a
sufficiently saturated elementary extension $\mathbb U$.
They are restricted to $\widetilde{\C}$ only after uniform
representatives and their witnesses have been obtained.

For every $\gamma\in\Gamma(\mathbb U)^n$,
the base generated by $F$ and $\gamma$ is
$(\VF,\Gamma)$-generated. Apply Theorem~\ref{main:prop:HK}{} over that
base to choose RV representatives $\bm U_\gamma$ and actual
definable bijections
\[
 H_\gamma:A_\gamma\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathbb L\bm U_\gamma.
\]
These are equalities of positive classes, before groupification.
We retain the grading, the finite-to-one structure maps and
their finite-fiber bounds in the representative data.

Here is the precise compactness argument. Fix formulas for one
such representative and its bijection, with only the indicated
parameter $\gamma$ in addition to parameters from $F$.
For these formulas, being valid representative data is a
first-order condition on $\gamma$: it asserts the chosen sort
domains, totality of the structure maps, the specified finite-fiber
bounds, and that the graph of $H_\gamma$ defines a bijection with
the indicated domain and codomain.
The conditions from all choices cover the entire parameter
space in the saturated extension. Compactness gives a finite
subcover. Its finite Boolean refinement is an $F$-definable
partition on each piece of which the RV arities, label degrees,
representatives and bijection witnesses are given by fixed
formulas. Empty fibers are included with empty representatives.

Using tags for the finitely many pieces and degrees, write
$\mathcal U=\{(\gamma,u):u\in U_\gamma\}$ for the resulting
definable RV family; the structure maps remain part of its data.
In the inflated families, distinguish
\[
 A^{\mathrm{inf}}
   =\{(x,a):(\vv(x),a)\in\mathcal A\},
 \qquad
 U^{\mathrm{inf}}
   =\{(z,u):(\vrv(z),u)\in\mathcal U\},
\]
where $x\in(\VF^\times)^n$ and $z\in(\RV^\times)^n$.
Thus the first construction uses the VF value fibers and the
second uses the RV value fibers. The uniform bijections
$H_\gamma$ justify replacement of the inflated total VF space
by the corresponding family of lifts. Restricting all this
data to the Puiseux field yields the required representatives
at rational parameters.

Put $R=\sggk\RES$ and $g=[\G_m]\in R$.

\begin{thm*}[Inflated-family Fubini]
There is a finite $F$-definable partition $(E_a)_a$ of $\Q^n$
and classes $v_a\in R$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{trop:fub:cons}
 \nu(\gamma):=\bb E_b\!\left(\int[A_\gamma]\right)=v_a
 \quad\text{for }\gamma\in E_a.
\end{equation}
The partition includes the parameters with empty fibers, where
the value is $0$. Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{fubini:inflated:RES}
 \bb E_b\!\left(\int[A^{\mathrm{inf}}]\right)
   =g^n\sum_a\chi_b(E_a)v_a\quad\text{in }R.
\end{equation}
Applying the approved comparison $\Theta$ gives
\[
 \Vol([A^{\mathrm{inf}}])
   =[\G_m]^n\sum_a\chi_b(E_a)\Theta(v_a).
\]
\end{thm*}

\begin{proof}
\emph{A relative monomial decomposition.}
Work first on a fixed zero-coordinate stratum of one tagged
component, and write
$\mathcal U\subseteq\Gamma^n\times(\RV^\times)^r$.
We claim that it has a finite definable partition
$(\mathcal U_i)_i$, integer matrices $N_i\in\GL_r(\Z)$,
fixed nonempty RES sets $W_i$ with singleton value support,
and definable sets
$J_i\subseteq\Gamma^n\times\Gamma^{\ell_i}$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{relative:monomial:chart}
 h_{N_i}(U_{i,\gamma})
       =W_i\times(J_{i,\gamma})^\sharp,\qquad
 J_{i,\gamma}=\{\eta:(\gamma,\eta)\in J_i\}.
\end{equation}
All these data are over $F$, and the coordinate changes act on
$u$ alone, leaving the parameter $\gamma$ unchanged.
The finite labels are transported with the charts as in
Section~3. Combine the finitely many tag and zero strata at
the end.

Here is a proof of the claim from Lemma~5.12.
Apply that lemma to $X=U^{\mathrm{inf}}$, in coordinates
$(z,u)$, to get value-induced pieces
\[
 X_i=X\cap\vrv^{-1}(D_i),\qquad
 h_M(X_i)=V\times I^\sharp,\qquad \vrv(V)=\{\beta\}.
\]
Fix one such piece and let $m$ be the number of coordinates of
$V$. Each $X_i$ is invariant under multiplication of $z$ by
$(\K^\times)^n$: both the family condition and $D_i$
depend on $z$ only through its values.

Let $B$ be the block of $M$ in its first $m$ rows and first
$n$ columns, and put $p=\operatorname{rank}_{\Q}B$,
$h=m-p$. The induced action on $V$ has exponent matrix $B$.
Smith reduction gives $Q\in\GL_m(\Z)$ such that, after this
change of active coordinates, its image is
\[
 \{1\}^h\times(\K^\times)^p.
\]
Indeed the nonzero diagonal powers in Smith form are
surjective on the units of the algebraically closed residue
field. Since the remaining factor $I^\sharp$ already contains
every angular coordinate, invariance of $X_i$ implies
invariance of $V$ under this image. It follows that
\[
 h_Q(V)=W_i\times(\beta_{\mathrm{free}})^\sharp
\]
for a definable $W_i$ in the first $h$ fixed-value
coordinates.

The first $h$ rows of $\operatorname{diag}(Q,1)M$ vanish in
the $z$ columns. Their restrictions to the $u$ columns form
a primitive sublattice of $\Z^r$: their span is primitive
already in $\Z^{n+r}$, since these rows belong to an integer
basis. Extend these rows to a basis of $\Z^r$, obtaining
$N_i\in\GL_r(\Z)$. Write
\[
 h_{N_i}(u)=(w,y),\qquad
 \vrv(W_i)=\{\beta_i^0\},\qquad \ell_i=r-h.
\]
On a fixed joint value tuple, membership in $X_i$ is now
equivalent to $w\in W_i$. All other angular coordinates
are free by the preceding invariance argument.
Consequently the required value set is
\[
 J_i=\{(\gamma,\eta):
       (\gamma,N_i^{-1}(\beta_i^0,\eta))\in D_i\}.
\]
This proves (\ref{relative:monomial:chart}).
It also proves original-base definability: $W_i$ is a
definable projection, its value tuple is fixed over $F$,
and all coordinate matrices are integral. No angular
representative is retained as a parameter.

\emph{A separate base partition.}
The projections of the value pieces to the parameter space
may overlap. For example, in the constant family
$U_\gamma=\{1,2\rv(t),3\rv(t)\}$, the natural pieces at
internal values $0$ and $1$ both project onto the whole base.
We therefore index the source pieces by $i$ and construct
the base pieces independently.

For each $i$, put $e_i(\gamma)=\chi_b(J_{i,\gamma})$.
These are finite-range constructible integer functions.
For completeness, quantifier elimination and
inclusion--exclusion express the indicator of a definable
subset of $\Q^{n+\ell}$ as a finite integer combination of
indicators of closed rational polyhedra.
Every nonempty closed polyhedron has bounded Euler
characteristic $1$: its sufficiently large box cuts are
nonempty closed bounded polyhedra, with geometric Euler
characteristic $1$. A projection and a nonempty fiber of
a closed polyhedron are closed polyhedra again.
Thus for its indicator the fiber integral is the indicator
of the projection. Linearity proves finite-range
constructibility and the projection identity
\begin{equation}\label{gamma:projection:formula}
 \chi_b(J_i)=\int_{\Q^n} e_i(\gamma)\,d\chi_b.
\end{equation}

Take the common finite Boolean refinement of all the supports
$\operatorname{pr}_{\Gamma^n}(J_i)$ and the level sets of
all $e_i$. This is the partition $(E_a)_a$.
It includes the complement of the union of those supports.
Write $e_{ia}$ for the value of $e_i$ on $E_a$, and set
\[
 c_i=[W_i]g^{\ell_i},\qquad v_a=\sum_i e_{ia}c_i\quad\text{in }R.
\]
For every rational $\gamma$, all its coordinates are
$F$-definable. The fiber representatives and
(\ref{relative:monomial:chart}), evaluated by the retraction
of Section~4, give
\[
 \bb E_b\!\left(\int[A_\gamma]\right)
       =\sum_i\chi_b(J_{i,\gamma})[W_i]g^{\ell_i}.
\]
This proves (\ref{trop:fub:cons}), including empty fibers.
Every contribution indexed by $i$ is constant on each $E_a$;
their projections themselves are not required to be a partition.

\emph{The total inflated space.}
Give $U^{\mathrm{inf}}$ the label
$(z,f_{\vrv(z)}(u))$, where $f_\gamma$ is the retained
label of $\bm U_\gamma$; keep the finite tags and degrees.
Its lift is definably bijective to $A^{\mathrm{inf}}$:
use $H_{\vv(x)}$ on the second factor and retain $x$.
The base RV tag is then $z=\rv(x)$. Thus this replacement
is justified by an actual uniform bijection.

The relative coordinate changes send the corresponding pieces
of $U^{\mathrm{inf}}$ to
\[
 W_i\times J_i^\sharp,
\]
after a coordinate permutation. Here $J_i^\sharp$ is the
full RV lift in $n+\ell_i$ coordinates.
The Section~4 product formula therefore gives directly
\[
 \bb E_b\!\left(\int[A^{\mathrm{inf}}]\right)
   =\sum_i\chi_b(J_i)[W_i]g^{n+\ell_i}
   =g^n\sum_i\chi_b(J_i)c_i.
\]
Using (\ref{gamma:projection:formula}) and the base partition,
\[
 \sum_i\chi_b(J_i)c_i
     =\sum_a\chi_b(E_a)\sum_i e_{ia}c_i
     =\sum_a\chi_b(E_a)v_a.
\]
This proves (\ref{fubini:inflated:RES}). Applying $\Theta$
proves the displayed motivic-volume identity.
\end{proof}

The factor $[\G_m]^n$ comes from the additional $n$ VF value
coordinates in $A^{\mathrm{inf}}$.
For $A_\gamma=B\cap\vv^{-1}(\gamma)$, the formula above still
concerns this inflated total space.
An identity for the original $B$ requires a decomposition
compatible with its own tropicalization; it does not follow by
cancelling $[\G_m]^n$ in the Grothendieck ring.

In $U=\bigcup_\gamma\gamma^\sharp\times U_\gamma$, the twistbacks at internal values $0$ and $1$ have respectively one and two points in the last coordinate. Thus they must lie in different twistoid pieces, whose projections to the base overlap at every $\gamma$. \begin{rem}[The polyhedral base pieces]
Each $E_a\subseteq\Q^n$ is the intersection with $\Q^n$ of
a finite Boolean combination of closed rational polyhedra in
$\R^n$. Here a closed rational polyhedron is specified by
finitely many affine inequalities
\[
 a_j\cdot x\geq c_j,\qquad a_j\in\Q^n,\quad c_j\in\Q.
\]
In particular, the $E_a$ need not themselves be closed
polyhedra; $(0,1)\cap\Q$ is a simple example.
The finite arrangement of the bounding hyperplanes refines
them into relatively open rational polyhedral cells, including
cells of lower dimension. Such a refinement preserves the
preceding formula by additivity of $\chi_b$ and constancy of
the coefficients on each original base piece.

The constants $c_j$ may be nonzero. If the chosen defining
conditions are homogeneous, the resulting pieces can instead
be refined into relatively open rational polyhedral cones.
This conditional observation about the value-group formulas
does not change the standing assumption that
$\Gamma(\mathbb S)$ is nontrivial.
\end{rem}

% BEGIN REFEREE INSERTION R086
\ResolvedRefereeRemark{086}{Error}{Polyhedra versus constructible polyhedral sets}{%
\textit{Original source line 1844.}\quad A definable set in $\Gamma^n$ need not be an intersection of closed half-spaces; $(0,1)$ is already a counterexample. Replace this by ``finite Boolean combinations of rational polyhedra'', or refine to relatively open rational polyhedral cells. Also say inequalities, rather than equations, when defining half-spaces. Over the trivially valued base the defining linear conditions are homogeneous, but the resulting pieces can be relatively open cones or Boolean combinations of cones. This is the convention used in \cite[\S~2.2]{Nic:Pay:trop:fub}.
}{The author approved the replacement on November 13, 2026. The base pieces are traces of finite Boolean combinations of closed rational polyhedra and admit relatively open cell refinements. The conic observation is conditional on homogeneous defining conditions and does not change the standing nontrivial-value-group hypothesis.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R086


\subsection{Fubini theorem}
In this subsection, return to an arbitrary
$(\VF,\Gamma)$-generated ground substructure $\mathbb S$,
with the standing nontriviality of $\Gamma(\mathbb S)$.
All definability remains in the original RV language.
Let $A,A'\in\VF_*$ and let
$f:A\to B$, $f':A'\to B$ be definable maps.
Write $A_b=f^{-1}(b)$, $A'_b=(f')^{-1}(b)$ and
$\mathbb S_b=\mathbb S\langle b\rangle$.
For a parameter base $\mathbb T$, write
\[
 R(\mathbb T)=\sggk(\RES_{\mathbb T})
\]
for the ungraded RES quotient computed with
$\mathbb T$-definable sets, maps and value-fiber relations.
The subscript $\mathrm b$ in $\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}$ denotes
the bounded retraction; the parameter base is specified
separately.

\begin{thm}\label{fubini}
Suppose that for every $b\in B$ there are
$\mathbb S_b$-definable objects
$\bm U_b,\bm U'_b\in\RV[*]$ and
$\mathbb S_b$-definable bijections
\[
 H_b:A_b\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathbb L\bm U_b,
 \qquad
 H'_b:A'_b\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathbb L\bm U'_b
\]
such that
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U_b])
 =
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U'_b])
 \quad\text{in }R(\mathbb S_b).
\]
Then, over the original ground $\mathbb S$,
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S}\!\left(\int[A]\right)
 =
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S}\!\left(\int[A']\right)
 \quad\text{in }R(\mathbb S).
\]
As usual, $B$ and its points are interpreted in the sufficiently
saturated ambient model.
\end{thm}

The lifting hypotheses mean
\[
 [A_b]_+=[\mathbb L\bm U_b]_+,\qquad
 [A'_b]_+=[\mathbb L\bm U'_b]_+
\]
in the positive Grothendieck semiring over $\mathbb S_b$.
For these categories of definable sets, finite scissor
decompositions assemble into actual definable bijections;
the displayed maps specify the required witnesses explicitly.
The ground-generation assumption supplies the domain of
the global integral from Section~3.
No generation assumption is imposed on every $\mathbb S_b$
merely to evaluate the displayed RV retractions.

If ring equalities are intended instead, include and uniformize their stabilization objects before cancelling them at the end. The saturated interpretation of $b$ is already covered by the standing convention, and the coefficient base is clarified in Remark~\ref{rem:volb}. Also make explicit that the ground base satisfies the $(\VF,\Gamma)$-generation hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{main:prop:HK}, which is needed for the displayed conclusion involving $\int$.

\begin{rem}\label{rem:volb}
Whenever $\mathbb S_b$ is $(\VF,\Gamma)$-generated, the fiber
integral of Section~3 is available and the hypothesis can
equivalently be written
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S_b}
       \!\left(\int[A_b]\right)
 =
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S_b}
       \!\left(\int[A'_b]\right).
\]
This applies, for example, to a base parameter $b$ consisting
of VF and Gamma coordinates.
If $B$ is definably bijective over $\mathbb S$ to a set of such
tuples, transport the parameter along that bijection:
the two parameter bases have the same definable closure,
so their definable categories and the stated equalities agree.
This does not assert equality of their generated substructures.

For $F=\C((t))$, the conclusion over the ground base may be
transported by the already constructed comparison $\Theta$:
it becomes $\Vol([A])=\Vol([A'])$.
The hypothesis still concerns every $b\in B$ in the
saturated ambient model, with its own coefficient base
$F\langle b\rangle$.
It is not enough to test only the points of $B$ in the
Puiseux field.

For arbitrary $b$, retain the intrinsic ring
$R(F\langle b\rangle)$.
An algebraically closed ambient valued field does not enlarge
the definability base.
In particular, adjoining an algebraic closure of the residue
field to the coefficient field of a realization can lose
arithmetic descent; an action recording only tame inertia
does not restore it.

Here is a concrete instance.
Choose a unit $b$ with residue $s$ transcendental over $\C$
and put $K=F(b)$ with its Gauss valuation.
For a polynomial $\sum_i a_i b^i$, its terms of least
$t$-valuation have nonzero residual polynomial in $s$.
Consequently
\[
 k(K)=k=\C(s),\qquad \Gamma(K)=\Z.
\]
The residue set
\[
 L=\{z:z^2=s\}
\]
is defined over $K$.
It is a nonsplit quadratic residue cover: $s$ is not a square
in $\C(s)$, since its order at $s=0$ is odd.
Over $\overline{k}$ it becomes two points, each with trivial
tame-inertia action, just as the constant two-point set does.

The distinction survives in the RES quotient.
Indeed, at this Gauss base the same finite framing argument
used for $\Theta$ normalizes every finite rational value
support by roots of $t$.
RV quantifier elimination gives constructible sets and graphs
over $k$, with a finite cyclic inertia action and with their
$k$-descent retained.
Changing a framing root multiplies coordinates by roots of
unity in $\C$, so it gives a $k$-defined isomorphism after
forgetting inertia.
Applying compactly supported \'etale Euler realization then
gives an additive and multiplicative invariant
\[
 \rho:R(K)\longrightarrow
 K_0\bigl(\operatorname{Rep}_{\Q_\ell}(G_k)\bigr),
 \qquad G_k=\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{k}/k).
\]
Here the realization is
$\sum_j(-1)^j[H_c^j(-_{\overline{k}},\Q_\ell)]$;
its additivity and multiplicativity follow from the
localization sequence and K\"unneth formula.\footnote{For
the standard realization on $K_0(\var_k)$, see
J.~Nicaise, \emph{A trace formula for varieties over a
discretely valued field}, Section~2.1(2),
\href{https://arxiv.org/pdf/0805.1323}{arXiv:0805.1323}.
Only this standard cohomological realization is used in this
diagnostic; no comparison theorem for RES is assumed.}
The quotient relations are killed because both
$q^\sharp$ and $\G_m$ normalize to $\G_m$ after inertia is
forgotten. Thus this argument constructs the required
invariant without asserting a new comparison isomorphism.
If $\varepsilon$ is the quadratic character attached to
$k(\sqrt{s})/k$, then
\[
 \rho([L])=[1]+[\varepsilon]\ne 2[1].
\]
For $g\in G_k$ with $\varepsilon(g)=-1$, the two traces
are $0$ and $2$. Residue base change gives two inertia-fixed
points in both cases, so the proposed comparison is not
injective.

There is also no restriction of the theorem's test to points
generating unramified extensions of $F$.
For example, take
\[
 B=\{b:b^2=t\},\qquad A=B,\qquad f=\mathrm{id}_B,
 \qquad A'=\varnothing.
\]
Every $b\in B$ has value $1/2$, so $F(b)/F$ is ramified
quadratic. The unramified-point test has no points to check.
On the other hand, $\rv$ identifies $B$ with the finite
RES object
\[
 T=\{u:u^2=\rv(t)\}\in\RES[0].
\]
The two roots have distinct RV classes; conversely these are
all the roots of $u^2=\rv(t)$.
The degree-zero lifting comparison therefore gives
\[
 \Vol([B])=\Theta([T])
   =[\{x:x^2=1\},\ \zeta\cdot x=\zeta x].
\]
The action factors through $\mu_2$ and interchanges the two
points. Forgetting the action and taking compactly supported
Euler characteristic sends this class to $2$, so it is
nonzero. This Euler characteristic respects the linear-action
relations, since every affine space of a fixed dimension has
Euler characteristic $1$.
Thus the asserted unramified-only criterion would give a
false conclusion. The theorem retains the test at all
ambient parameters in their original RES coefficient rings.

% BEGIN REFEREE INSERTION R088
\ResolvedRefereeRemark{088}{Error; revised}{Arithmetic residue descent is missing}{%
\textit{Original source line 1862.}\quad The proposed twistback/base-change comparison does not retain residue-field descent for arbitrary $b$. Take a unit $b$ with residue $s$ transcendental over $\mathbb C$, endowed with the Gauss valuation. Then $k(\mathbb C((t))(b))=\mathbb C(s)$ and its value group is $\mathbb Z$. The residue object $\{z:z^2=s\}$ has a nontrivial arithmetic Galois action over $\mathbb C(s)$, but its base change to $\mathbb C_b$ is two points with trivial tame-inertia action. Their classes are distinguished before base change by the arithmetic Galois representation on cohomology. Thus the proposed comparison with only $\hat\mu^{r_b}$-action is not an isomorphism. Keep $\sggk\RES$ over $\mathbb S\langle b\rangle$, or construct a target retaining residue-field descent as well as inertia. Taking an algebraic closure as the ambient model does not enlarge the definability base.
}{The author approved the intrinsic fiber RES target and the replacement on November 13, 2026. The Gauss-base quadratic residue cover retains arithmetic descent in the RES quotient, as its Galois realization shows. The unsupported general residue-base-change comparison and local Vol notation are removed; the global comparison over C((t)) is retained.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R088

% BEGIN REFEREE INSERTION R089
\ResolvedRefereeRemark{089}{Error}{The unramified-only test is false as stated}{%
\textit{Original source line 1863.}\quad The last assertion has a simple counterexample in the general definable setting of the theorem. Put $B=\{b:b^2=t\}$, $A=B$, $f=\mathrm{id}_B$, and $A'=\varnothing$. Every point of $B$ generates a ramified quadratic extension of $\mathbb C((t))$, so the proposed test only at unramified points is vacuous. Nevertheless $\operatorname{Vol}(A)$ is the nonzero class of the two-element monodromy set, whose ordinary Euler characteristic is $2$. Delete this assertion, or state and prove a different test on a specifically constructed auxiliary residue base. It cannot be imposed directly on the original points $b\in B$.
}{The author approved deletion of the unramified-only test. The ramified quadratic parameter example has nonzero degree-zero monodromy volume while the restricted test is vacuous. The hypothesis continues to be tested at every ambient parameter over its original coefficient base.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R089
\end{rem}



The following fact shall be needed:

\begin{lem}\label{special:term:constant:disc}
Let $F_i(x) = F_i(x_1, \ldots, x_n)$ be a finite list of polynomials with coefficients in $\VF(\bb S)$. Let $A \sub \VF^n$ be a definable set.  Then there is a definable bijection $T : A \fun A^\flat$ such that $A^\flat$ is an $\RV$-pullback and  every composite function $F_i \circ T^{-1}$ is $\rv$-contractible, that is, for each $i$ there is a commutative diagram
\[
\bfig
  \square(0,0)/`->`->`->/<1000,400>[A^\flat`\VF`\rv(A^\flat)`\RV_\infty;
  `\rv`\rv`(F_i \circ  T^{-1})_{\downarrow}]
  \morphism(0,400)<500,0>[A^\flat`A; T^{-1}]
  \morphism(500,400)<500,0>[A`\VF; F_i]
 \efig
\]
\end{lem}
This lemma follows from \cite[Theorem~5.5]{Yin:special:trans}, cited as Lemma~\ref{special:bi:term:constant} below, where $T$ may be taken to be a so-called special bijection, as in Definition~\ref{defn:special:bijection} but much simpler (we shall not need this here, though, because no volume forms are involved).

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{fubini}]
By partitioning $B$ if necessary, it is enough to prove the claim either when $f$, $f'$ are both surjective or when $f$ is surjective and $A'$ is empty. We will  deal with the first case, the second being similar but simpler (just skip all the steps that involve $A'$ in the argument below).

%Write  $\int [A_b] = [\bm U_{b}] /  (\bm P - 1)$ and $\int [A'_b] = [\bm U'_{b}] /  (\bm P - 1)$.

After the initial partition by the images of $f$ and $f'$,
compactness gives a finite definable partition $(B_i)_i$
such that the two RV representatives, their structure maps
and finite-fiber bounds, and both bijection graphs
$H_b,H'_b$ are all given by fixed formulas on each $B_i$.
Indeed validity of each fixed finite package is a first-order
condition, and these conditions cover the parameter space
in the saturated model. Choose quantifier-free formulas
$\xi_i(x,y)$, $\xi'_i(x,y')$ for the two representative objects.

For the graph families over $B_i$, the replacements are the
explicit definable bijections
\[
 (b,a)\longmapsto(b,H_b(a)),\qquad
 (b,a')\longmapsto(b,H'_b(a')).
\]
The base coordinate and all finite RV labels are retained.
The further preparation and descent of the RES equality
witnesses belong to the subsequent argument.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Preparation with the RV coordinates retained.}
By the standing convention, write
$B\subseteq\VF^{d_0}\times\RV_\infty^{q_0}$.
First replace $A,A'$ by the graphs of $f,f'$.
Their projections back to $A,A'$ are definable bijections,
and the graphs still belong to $\VF_*$.
On each compactness piece, write
$\bm U_b=(U_b,p_b)$ and $\bm U'_b=(U'_b,p'_b)$,
with fixed label degrees $k,k'$.
All the RV coordinates of $u\in U_b$, including the finite
tags, remain part of the representative.

Apply the preceding preparation lemma to the finite list of
VF polynomials appearing in the defining formulas for $B$,
the representative sets and their structure-map graphs.
These polynomials involve the VF base variables and ground
coefficients; the RV parameters remain free variables.
Thus preparation can be performed on the VF projection of
the base. Carry along the original RV coordinates and every
new RV coordinate introduced by the preparation.
RV contractibility of the prepared polynomials makes all
these RV-sorted data depend on the prepared VF base only
through its RV tuple.

After reparametrizing, and partitioning the zero-coordinate
strata, write the base as
\[
 B^\flat=\{(c,t):\tau=(\rv(c),t)\in S\},\qquad
 S\subseteq(\RV^\times)^d\times\RV_\infty^q.
\]
Here $c$ has $d$ nonzero VF coordinates and $t$ contains
all the retained RV coordinates; the finite stratum tags
are retained as well.
The inverse preparation recovers the original parameter
$b=b(c,t)$.
The representatives and label maps are now denoted by
\[
 \bm U_\tau=(U_\tau,p_\tau),\qquad
 p_\tau:U_\tau\longrightarrow(\RV^\times)^k,
\]
and similarly for the primed family.
Using the transported lift bijections, the first total space
is definably bijective to
\[
 Y=\{(c,t,x,u):
       \tau=(\rv(c),t)\in S,\ u\in U_\tau,\
       \rv(x)=p_\tau(u)\}.
\]
The primed construction is identical.
These total spaces belong to $\VF_*$: they are obtained by
the displayed bijections from the original graph families,
and RV-fiber dimension is invariant under definable
bijections.

Put $\tau=(s,t)$ and form the RV object
\[
 W=\{(\tau,u):\tau\in S,\ u\in U_\tau\},\qquad
 q(\tau,u)=(s,p_\tau(u)).
\]
Its structure map $q$ is finite-to-one.
Indeed, fix $(s,w)$ and choose any
$c\in\rv^{-1}(s)$ and $x\in\rv^{-1}(w)$.
Then $q^{-1}(s,w)$ identifies, by keeping $(t,u)$, with the
RV fiber of $Y$ at the fixed VF tuple $(c,x)$.
That fiber is finite because $Y\in\VF_*$.
The choices of $c,x$ are used only to prove finiteness and
are not parameters in the definition of $W$ or $q$.
Thus $\bm W=(W,q)\in\RV[d+k]$.

There is an explicit definable bijection
\[
 \mathbb L\bm W\longrightarrow Y,\qquad
 ((c,x),((s,t),u))\longmapsto(c,t,x,u).
\]
Its inverse restores the forced coordinate $s=\rv(c)$.
Define $\bm W'$ in the same way, of degree $d+k'$.
Consequently the two global integrals are represented by
$\bm W,\bm W'$ modulo the lifting congruence.
The coordinates $t$ remain RV coordinates; they are not
replaced by VF coordinates or given additional lifts.

This preparation does not descend the fiber RES equality
from $\mathbb S\langle b(c,t)\rangle$ to
$\mathbb S\langle\tau\rangle$.
That equality is still interpreted over the former base.
Its descent requires the finite RES-relation witnesses in
the later argument; once those witnesses have been
uniformized, their formulas can be included in the same
finite preparation package.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Controlling the value parameters.}
For the subsequent RV calculations set $U=W$, $U'=W'$,
\[
 D=\vrv(U),\qquad D'=\vrv(U'),\qquad E=\vrv(S).
\]
Work componentwise on fixed zero-coordinate strata,
retaining their finite tags.
For $\tau\in S$, let $D_\tau=\vrv(U_\tau)$,
where the value map is taken on all remaining nonzero
coordinates of the underlying representative.
The label maps are retained separately.

We claim that a finite ground-definable partition
$S=\coprod_a S_a$ makes $D_\tau$ depend only on
$\gamma=\vrv(\tau)$ on each $S_a$.
More precisely, there are ground-definable DOAG sets
$\mathcal D_a\subseteq\vrv(S_a)\times\Gamma^r$ with
\[
 D_\tau=(\mathcal D_a)_{\vrv(\tau)}
       \qquad(\tau\in S_a).
\]
The same partition can be chosen for the primed family.

Here are the parameter details.
Stable embeddedness of $\Gamma$, followed by compactness,
first gives finitely many fixed DOAG formulas
$\Phi_\ell(\delta,z)$ such that every $D_\tau$ has the form
$\Phi_\ell(\Gamma^r,c)$ for some tuple $c$ in $\Gamma$.
Partition $S$ according to the first applicable formula.
For a fixed formula the set
\[
 P_\tau=\{c:\ \forall\delta,\
       (\delta\in D_\tau\ \Longleftrightarrow\
                    \Phi_\ell(\delta,c))\}
\]
is a nonempty definable set in a power of $\Gamma$.

Fix a ground-definable $\epsilon>0$ in $\Gamma$.
There is a definable selection $c(\tau)\in P_\tau$ using
only $\tau$ and $\epsilon$.
For completeness, in one ordered coordinate choose $0$
when available. Otherwise use the first positive component,
if one exists, reflecting the negative part if necessary.
Choose its left endpoint if included, the midpoint for an
open bounded interval, or its left endpoint plus $\epsilon$
for an open ray. The component endpoints are definable
from the set. Apply this rule successively to projections
and fibers for tuples. O-minimality makes these choices
uniformly definable. Hence the selection does not retain
any externally chosen Gamma parameter.

For a nonzero RV tuple $\tau$, RV quantifier elimination
gives the parameter-control identity
\[
 \dcl(\mathbb S,\tau)\cap\Gamma
 =
 \operatorname{span}_{\Q}
 \bigl(\dcl(\mathbb S)\cap\Gamma,\ \vrv(\tau)\bigr).
\]
To see the point, on each leading-term guard the value
conditions are rational linear conditions in the value
variables, with constants from the ground and the values
of RV monomials in $\tau$.
A uniquely defined Gamma element is a boundary value of
these conditions, and therefore has the displayed rational
affine form.
Apply this to every coordinate of $c(\tau)$.

These rational-affine descriptions cover every ambient
$\tau$; compactness makes the list finite.
Refine $S$ by their equality loci and by the earlier formula
choices. On each resulting piece,
\[
 c(\tau)=L_a\vrv(\tau)+\alpha_a,
 \qquad L_a\text{ a rational matrix},\quad
 \alpha_a\in(\dcl(\mathbb S)\cap\Gamma)^{\dim c}.
\]
Consequently one may take
\[
 \mathcal D_a=
 \{(\gamma,\delta):\gamma\in\vrv(S_a),\
             \Phi_{\ell(a)}(\delta,L_a\gamma+\alpha_a)\}.
\]
This is defined over the original ground.
Intersect the two finite partitions to handle the primed
family simultaneously.
On a selected base piece the notation
$D_{\vrv(\tau)}$, $D'_{\vrv(\tau)}$ is therefore justified.
The first partition may depend on angular data in $\tau$;
only after that partition do the value fibers depend on
$\vrv(\tau)$ alone.

% BEGIN REFEREE INSERTION R090
\ResolvedRefereeRemark{090}{Gap}{RV coordinates cannot simply be discarded}{%
\textit{Original source line 1894.}\quad The fiberwise argument retains the $\RV$-parameter $t$; compactness makes the resulting maps uniform but does not remove that coordinate. In particular an infinite $\RV$-set does not admit a definable injection into a power of $\VF$ (see \cite[Lemma~3.41]{hrushovski:kazhdan:integration:vf}). Thus the asserted reduction to $A,A',B$ all lying in $\VF$ needs a separate argument. A possible repair is to carry the $\RV$-coordinates and the finite-to-one structure maps throughout the proof, checking that all total spaces remain objects of the relevant categories; otherwise restrict the theorem to a $\VF$ base and prove the extra coding needed for its total spaces.
}{The author approved the mixed-base preparation on November 13, 2026. All original and newly introduced RV coordinates, finite tags and structure maps are retained. The combined RV structure map has finite fibers by its identification with actual RV fibers of the total mixed lift. No injection of an RV base into VF or extra lifting of its RV coordinates is used.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R090

% BEGIN REFEREE INSERTION R091
\ResolvedRefereeRemark{091}{Clarification; revised}{Justification of the value-parameter dependence}{%
\textit{Original source line 1896.}\quad The finite partition asserted here is compatible with the usual parameter-control argument, but stable embeddedness alone is not its justification. Use $\dcl(\mathbb S,t)\cap\Gamma=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb Q}(\dcl(\mathbb S)\cap\Gamma,\vrv(t))$, together with the piecewise rational-affine description of definable value-group data. After finitely partitioning $S$, this gives the required dependence only on $\vrv(t)$. This is the precise replacement for the justification requested in the human report; it does not contradict the assertion after partitioning.

RV quantifier elimination and compactness give finitely many rational-affine descriptions. After their common finite partition the value supports depend only on the values of the RV parameters. RES-equality witness descent remains a separate later step.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R091

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Support notation and further refinements.}
For a definable family of value supports put
\[
 D_{\gamma,i}=\{\delta:(\gamma,\delta)\in D_i\},\qquad
 D_i=\{(\gamma,\delta):\gamma\in E,\ \delta\in D_{\gamma,i}\}.
\]
For the primed family the corresponding notation is
$D'_{\gamma,i'}$ and $(D'_{i'})_{i'}$.
Each function $\gamma\mapsto\chi_b(D_{\gamma,i})$ has
finite range and definable level sets.
A common finite partition of $E$ makes all these functions,
and their primed counterparts, constant. On one such piece,
write the constants as $e_i,e'_{i'}$; Gamma Fubini gives
\[
 \chi_b(D_i)=e_i\chi_b(E),\qquad
 \chi_b(D'_{i'})=e'_{i'}\chi_b(E).
\]
Whenever the source or the base is restricted further,
we refine again by the level sets of the resulting fiber
Euler functions.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{A relative Gamma-push calculation.}
We use the following calculation over the original ground
in place of choosing a common fixed-value residue fiber
of the base.
Let $\bm X=(X,p)\in\RV[k]$, let
$J\subseteq X\times\Gamma^r$ be definable, and put
\[
 J_x=\{\xi:(x,\xi)\in J\},\qquad
 Y=\{(x,z):x\in X,\ \vrv(z)\in J_x\}.
\]
Give $Y$ the finite-to-one label $(p(x),z)$, of degree
$k+r$. Set $g=[\G_m]$ in $R(\mathbb S)$.
The integer function $c(x)=\chi_b(J_x)$ has finite range
and definable level sets $X_e=\{x:c(x)=e\}$, and
\begin{equation}\label{relative:Gamma:push}
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S}([Y])
       =g^r\sum_e e\,
               \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S}([X_e]).
\end{equation}
The sum is finite. All classes in this formula use the
ungraded bounded retraction; the specified RV labels are
retained before that evaluation.
Two finite-to-one labels on the same RV set give the same
bounded value: pad them with constant-one coordinates to
a common degree. The underlying identity is then an RV
isomorphism. Each padding multiplies by the degree-one
point class, whose bounded value is $1$.

Here is a proof. First work on fixed zero-coordinate
strata, retaining the finite tags.
Apply the preceding parameter-control argument to the
Gamma fibers $J_x$, treating $x$ as the RV parameter.
After a finite partition of $X$, the set $J_x$ depends only
on $\vrv(x)$ through a fixed ground-definable Gamma
family. Ordinary Gamma fiber integration now proves
finite-range constructibility of $c$.
Intersect this partition with its level sets.

Apply Lemma 5.12 on each resulting part and write one chart
as
\[
 h_M(X_0)=V\times I^\sharp,\qquad
 \vrv(V)=\{\beta\},\qquad I\subseteq\Gamma^\ell.
\]
Here $\ell$ denotes the number of free RV coordinates,
rather than the dimension of the Gamma set $I$.
Because $M$ is integral unimodular, the original values
are $M^{-1}(\beta,\eta)$ when $\eta=\vrv(w)$.
The corresponding part of $Y$ therefore has the chart
\[
 V\times K^\sharp,\qquad
 K=\{(\eta,\xi):\eta\in I,\
            \xi\in J_{M^{-1}(\beta,\eta)}\}.
\]
The subscript on $J$ here denotes the value-parametrized
Gamma family on this selected part.
If $c=e$ there, Gamma Fubini gives
$\chi_b(K)=e\chi_b(I)$.
The monomial evaluation of the bounded retraction is
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([Y_0])
      =[V]\,g^{\ell+r}\chi_b(K)
      =g^r e\,[V]\,g^\ell\chi_b(I)
      =g^r e\,\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([X_0]).
\]
The coordinate change on $Y_0$ is $h_M$ on $x$ and the
identity on $z$, so it preserves the finite labels by
transport. Summing the finitely many parts proves
(\ref{relative:Gamma:push}), entirely over the original
ground.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Keep the residue families over their RV base.}
Once the finite monomial witness packages have been
descended and prepared over $S$, write the resulting
fiber charts as
\[
 h_{M_i}(U_{\tau,i})
      =V_{i,\tau}\times I_{i,\tau}^{\sharp},
 \qquad \tau\in S.
\]
The matrices, arities, finite tags, label maps and graphs
are part of these packages.
After the preceding parameter control and a finite common
partition of $S$, the numbers
$\chi_b(I_{i,\tau})=e_i$ are constant on a selected base
piece; do the same for the primed family.
The affine map
$\eta\mapsto M_i^{-1}(\beta_{i,\tau},\eta)$ identifies
this free support with the original value piece, so their
bounded Euler characteristics agree.
Retain every source chart on that piece. A chart may have
empty fibers, and its projection need not equal the
projections of other charts.

Put
\[
 \mathcal V_i=\{(\tau,v):\tau\in S,\ v\in V_{i,\tau}\}.
\]
This is an RV object with a chosen finite-to-one label;
it need not be a RES object over the ground.
Apply (\ref{relative:Gamma:push}) with
$X=\mathcal V_i$ and Gamma fiber $I_{i,\tau}$.
The maps $(\tau,u)\mapsto(\tau,h_{M_i}(u))$
retain the RV base coordinate. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{fubini:family:coefficients}
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\bm W])
   =\sum_i e_i g^{\ell_i}
               \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal V_i]),
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\bm W'])
   =\sum_{i'} e'_{i'}g^{\ell'_{i'}}
               \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal V'_{i'}]).
\end{equation}
The product labels in the calculation can be replaced by
the transported labels of $\bm W,\bm W'$: the monomial
evaluation in the ungraded bounded target is independent
of that label choice, as in the earlier evaluation formula.

There is a useful special case for the later RES relations.
For any definable RV family
$\mathcal Z=\{(\tau,z):z\in Z_\tau\}$ and definable
$\alpha:S\to\Gamma^n$, set
\[
 T_{\alpha,\mathcal Z}
  =\{(\tau,z,u):(\tau,z)\in\mathcal Z,\
                        \vrv(u)=\alpha(\tau)\}.
\]
Its Gamma fibers are singletons, of bounded Euler
characteristic $1$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{fubini:relative:torsor}
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([T_{\alpha,\mathcal Z}])
      =g^n\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal Z])
      =\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([(\G_m)^n\times\mathcal Z]).
\end{equation}
This treats a varying value-torsor relation directly over
the RV base.

The remaining task is to obtain the finite monomial and
RES-relation witness packages over this RV base from the
hypothesis over $\mathbb S\langle b\rangle$.
The calculations above do not assert that descent.
They show how to use the descended packages: combine
(\ref{fubini:family:coefficients}) with
(\ref{fubini:relative:torsor}), additivity and the actual
bijections in the finite relation witnesses.
A single finite Boolean partition of the base handles
both families and all their supports. No selected chart
is required to project surjectively, and every torus
factor in these formulas is retained.

\ResolvedRefereeRemark{093}{Clarification; revised}{Explain the common refinement}{%
\textit{Original source line 1902.}\quad The common refinement can be justified more explicitly. First refine the value-group base so that the relevant projections of the $U_i$ to $S$ have the same value support. Their common Boolean refinement then consists of twistoids. Restrict each $U_i$ to a selected base piece; it remains a twistoid, with a fixed twistback projecting onto that base twistback. Apply Lemma~\ref{coh:decom} to these restrictions using $\Gamma$-cohesive refinements. Their projections to the selected base are now $\Gamma$-cohesive, so a final value-group refinement makes each surviving projection surjective. On that final refinement, also re-establish constancy of the fiber Euler characteristics $\chi_b(D_{\gamma,i})$. This supplies the compatibility used in the next paragraph; the earlier objection should not be read as an obstruction to the existence of the refinement.

It retains residue families over the existing RV base and replaces the common twistback, dotted-fiber and weighted fixed-fiber reduction. The proof keeps all torus factors, allows empty fibers and overlapping projections, and handles varying value torsors. The finite witness construction and VF-to-RV descent remain for R094--R095.}
% END REFEREE INSERTION R093

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Finite witnesses for the RES quotient.}
The following object-level criterion supplies the finite
data needed for compactness.

\begin{test}\label{test}
Let $\mathbb T$ be a parameter base and
$P,Q\in\RES_{\mathbb T}$.
The equality $[P]=[Q]$ in $R(\mathbb T)$ holds if and only if
there are $C\in\RES_{\mathbb T}$, finitely many ordered pairs
$(L_j,R_j)$, and an actual $\mathbb T$-definable bijection
\begin{equation}\label{finite:RES:witness}
 P\sqcup C\sqcup\bigsqcup_j R_j
       \ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\
 Q\sqcup C\sqcup\bigsqcup_j L_j .
\end{equation}
For each $j$, the pair $(L_j,R_j)$ is either
\[
 \bigl(\alpha_j^\sharp\times Z_j,\
              (\G_m)^{n_j}\times Z_j\bigr)
\]
or its reverse, where
$Z_j\in\RES_{\mathbb T}$ and
$\alpha_j\in\Gamma^{n_j}$ is $\mathbb T$-definable.
All unions and copies are tagged disjoint unions.
\end{test}

To prove the criterion, an element of the ideal defining
$R(\mathbb T)$ is a finite sum of its generators times
elements of $K_0(\RES_{\mathbb T})$.
Write each coefficient as a difference of two positive
object classes. Absorb those objects into $Z_j$, reversing
a pair for a negative coefficient.
The one-coordinate generators already suffice; tuple
generators give the same ideal by expanding a difference
of products.
Thus $[P]-[Q]=\sum_j([L_j]-[R_j])$ in the group completion.
Equality there means that adding one common positive class
$[C]$ makes the two positive sums equal.
Positive scissor equalities assemble into an actual
definable bijection, giving
(\ref{finite:RES:witness}).
Conversely that bijection and the defining ideal relations
imply $[P]=[Q]$ in $R(\mathbb T)$.
This gives finite object witnesses directly.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Choose the full packages before preparing the base.}
Return temporarily to $b\in B$ in the original parameter
coordinates, and put $\mathbb S_b=\mathbb S\langle b\rangle$.
Apply Lemma 5.12 to the two RV representatives over
$\mathbb S_b$, with their zero strata and finite tags retained.
The standing nontriviality of the ground Gamma group
also holds over $\mathbb S_b$.
Only RV decomposition and the RV retraction are used here;
no fiber VF-integration hypothesis is added.

For one such finite package the charts have matrices $M_i$,
free arities $\ell_i$, residue factors $V_{i,b}$,
Gamma supports $I_{i,b}$ and integers
$e_i=\chi_b(I_{i,b})$, and similarly for the primed family.
Set
\[
 C_{i,b}=V_{i,b}\times(\G_m)^{\ell_i},
 \qquad C'_{i',b}=V'_{i',b}\times(\G_m)^{\ell'_{i'}}.
\]
Writing $a^+=\max(a,0)$ and $a^-=\max(-a,0)$, form the
positive RES objects
\[
 P_b=\bigsqcup_i e_i^+ C_{i,b}
       \ \sqcup\ \bigsqcup_{i'}(e'_{i'})^- C'_{i',b},
\]
\[
 Q_b=\bigsqcup_i e_i^- C_{i,b}
       \ \sqcup\ \bigsqcup_{i'}(e'_{i'})^+ C'_{i',b}.
\]
Here $mC$ means $m$ tagged copies of $C$.
The monomial evaluation and the hypothesis imply
$[P_b]=[Q_b]$ in $R(\mathbb S_b)$.
Choose the full finite witness of Test~\ref{test} over
$\mathbb S_b$: the stabilizer $C_b$, the factors $Z_{j,b}$,
the value tuples $\alpha_j(b)$, the orientations of the
pairs, and the graph of the displayed bijection.

Include in the package the representative and label
formulas, their finite-fiber bounds, the monomial source
pieces and coordinate maps, the residue and Gamma factors,
and finite bounds for every RES value support.
Include formulas defining all Gamma-valued parameter
functions and the complete stabilization bijection graph.

The Euler integers also have finite first-order certificates.
After DOAG quantifier elimination, the indicator of each
$I_{i,b}$ is a finite integer combination of indicators
of closed rational polyhedra with $b$-definable Gamma
parameters. Its bounded Euler characteristic is the
same combination of their nonemptiness indicators,
since a nonempty closed polyhedron has bounded Euler
characteristic $1$.
Record those parameter formulas, the indicator identity
and the nonemptiness flags.
Validity of a fixed such certificate is first order.

Validity of the entire fixed finite package is now a
first-order condition on $b$: all arities and bounds are
fixed, and the required set identities, maps and actual
bijections are given by formulas.
These conditions cover every ambient $b\in B$.
Saturation and compactness therefore give a finite
definable partition of $B$ with one package on each piece.
In particular, the matrices, copy numbers, Euler integers,
arity lists and pair orientations are fixed on a piece.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Prepare the witness formulas themselves.}
Repeat the earlier mixed-base preparation with this larger
finite package.
First eliminate valued-field quantifiers in the formulas
for all RV/Gamma sets and maps in it.
Their only free VF variables are the VF coordinates of
the base parameter $b$. Apply the preparation lemma to
every VF polynomial occurring in these formulas, including
those for the stabilization bijection, the Gamma-valued
functions and the Euler certificates.
Carry every original and newly introduced RV coordinate.

As before, write the resulting base as
\[
 B^\flat=\{(c,t):\tau=(\rv(c),t)\in S\}.
\]
Every formula in the RV/Gamma witness package now depends
on $(c,t)$ only through $\tau$.
It therefore defines the representative sets, their label
maps, the monomial data, all auxiliary RES objects,
the Gamma functions $\alpha_j(\tau)$ and the actual
stabilization bijection over $\mathbb S\langle\tau\rangle$.
The finite support bounds and the Euler certificates remain
valid there: their defining first-order assertions involve
only $\tau$ after preparation.

This constructs the equality witness over the RV parameter
base. It does not deduce that equality from injectivity of
a base-extension map.
The lift bijections $H_b,H'_b$ are kept as transported
bijections of total mixed VF families; they need not
descend to RV maps.
Using them gives the total RV objects
$\bm W,\bm W'$ exactly as in the earlier mixed-coordinate
construction.
The Gamma functions and support sets may vary with
$\tau$; their constancy is not required.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Apply the finite witnesses to the total families.}
For every object family $F_\tau$ use the tagged total
\[
 \mathcal F=\{(\tau,u):\tau\in S,\ u\in F_\tau\}.
\]
Give each total a finite-to-one RV label, for example its
full coordinate tuple when necessary.
These totals are RV objects; no assertion that they are
ground RES objects is needed.
The uniform bijection (\ref{finite:RES:witness}) yields
an actual definable bijection
\[
 \mathcal P\sqcup\mathcal C\sqcup\bigsqcup_j\mathcal R_j
       \ \cong\
 \mathcal Q\sqcup\mathcal C\sqcup\bigsqcup_j\mathcal L_j .
\]
For each $j$, the relative torsor identity gives
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal L_j])
       =\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal R_j]),
\]
with either orientation, even for varying
$\alpha_j(\tau)$.
Apply the ungraded bounded retraction to the total
bijection and cancel the common summands additively:
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal P])
       =\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal Q]).
\]
The definitions of $P_\tau,Q_\tau$ turn their difference
into
\[
 \sum_i e_i g^{\ell_i}\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal V_i])
   -\sum_{i'}e'_{i'}g^{\ell'_{i'}}
                   \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\mathcal V'_{i'}]).
\]
By the preceding family-coefficient formula this is
$\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\bm W])
 -\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}([\bm W'])$.
Consequently the two global bounded values agree.
Since $\bm W,\bm W'$ represent the original total
VF objects under the lifting comparison, this proves
the conclusion of Theorem 5.17.
Sum over the finitely many base pieces.
All cancellations above take place in the additive
group of $R(\mathbb S)$; no torus class is inverted.

% END REFEREE INSERTION R095
\end{proof}



\begin{cor}\label{cor:global:vanishing}
Let $F=\C((t))$ and let $f:A\to B$ be a morphism of
varieties over $\C$.
Interpret these varieties in a sufficiently saturated
ambient model $\mathbb U$ of $\mathrm{ACVF}(0,0)$, using
finite affine charts.
For $b\in B(\mathbb U)$ put $A_b=f^{-1}(b)$ and
$\mathbb S_b=F\langle b\rangle$, with $b$ represented by its
VF chart coordinates.
If, for every $b\in B(\mathbb U)$,
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{\mathbb S_b}\!
       \left(\int[A_b]\right)=0
       \quad\text{in }R(\mathbb S_b),
\]
then $\Vol([A])=0$ in $\gmv$.
\end{cor}

\begin{proof}
Choose finite disjoint constructible pieces subordinate to
affine covers of $A$ and $B$ and encode their finite tags
by distinct elements of $\C$.
This realizes $f$ as a definable map between VF tuples.
Changing charts gives definably equivalent parameter
bases and transports the fiber equality.

Both $F$ and each $\mathbb S_b$ in these coordinates are
VF-generated. Thus the integrals of Section~3 are
available over these bases.
The lifting theorem supplies positive RV representatives
of $A_b$ with actual $\mathbb S_b$-definable lift bijections.
Apply Theorem~5.17 to $A\to B$ and the empty family
$\varnothing\to B$.
The displayed hypothesis is precisely the required
equality of their fiber retractions, so
\[
 \mathbb E_{\mathrm b}^{F}\!
       \left(\int[A]\right)=0
       \quad\text{in }R(F).
\]
Applying the global comparison $\Theta$ gives
$\Vol([A])=0$.
\end{proof}

% BEGIN APPROVED GEOMETRIC FUBINI
\begin{nota}\label{nota:geometric:fubini:target}
For a parameter base $\mathbb T$, put
\[
 R(\mathbb T)=\sggk\RES_{\mathbb T},\qquad
 R_g(\mathbb T)=R(\mathbb T)[L^{-1}],\qquad L=[\A].
\]
We use $\bb E_g^{\mathbb T}$ for the geometric retraction of Section~\ref{sec:retract}
followed by the natural homomorphism to $R_g(\mathbb T)$.
The RES grading is thus forgotten in the target. Write $\widetilde{\bb E}_g^{\mathbb T}$
for the unnormalized geometric Euler homomorphism followed by forgetting
the RES grading. If $\bm U\in\RV[k]$, then
\begin{equation}\label{geometric:fubini:normalization}
 \bb E_g^{\mathbb T}([\bm U])=L^{-k}\widetilde{\bb E}_g^{\mathbb T}([\bm U]).
\end{equation}
The class $L$ need not be a non-zero-divisor in $R(\mathbb T)$.

\end{nota}

\begin{prop}[Geometric version of Theorem~\ref{fubini}]\label{prop:geometric:fubini}
Let $\mathbb S$ be a $(\VF,\Gamma)$-generated ground substructure,
with $\Gamma(\mathbb S)$ nontrivial. Let $A,A'\in\VF_*$ and let
$f:A\to B$, $f':A'\to B$ be definable maps. Put
$A_b=f^{-1}(b)$, $A'_b=(f')^{-1}(b)$ and
$\mathbb S_b=\mathbb S\langle b\rangle$.
Suppose that for every $b\in B$ there are
$\mathbb S_b$-definable objects $\bm U_b,\bm U'_b\in\RV[*]$ and
$\mathbb S_b$-definable bijections
\[
 A_b\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\bb L\bm U_b,
 \qquad A'_b\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\bb L\bm U'_b
\]
such that
\[
 \bb E_g^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U_b])
 =\bb E_g^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U'_b])
 \quad\text{in }R_g(\mathbb S_b).
\]
Then
\[
 \bb E_g^{\mathbb S}\!\left(\int[A]\right)
 =\bb E_g^{\mathbb S}\!\left(\int[A']\right)
 \quad\text{in }R_g(\mathbb S).
\]
As in Theorem~\ref{fubini}, the hypothesis concerns all points of $B$ in the
sufficiently saturated ambient model.
\end{prop}

\begin{proof}
We explain the changes to the proof of Theorem~\ref{fubini}, retaining its
positive lifting witnesses and intrinsic parameter-relative RES quotients.

Fix $b$ temporarily, and let $k,k'$ be the degrees of its two RV
representatives. Write
\[
 a=\widetilde{\bb E}_g^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U_b]),\qquad
 a'=\widetilde{\bb E}_g^{\mathbb S_b}([\bm U'_b]).
\]
By the definition of localization, the equality $L^{-k}a=L^{-k'}a'$
is equivalent to the existence of an integer $N\geq k,k'$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{geometric:fubini:clearing}
 L^{N-k}a=L^{N-k'}a'\quad\text{in }R(\mathbb S_b).
\end{equation}
Indeed, after a common denominator has been chosen, some further power
of $L$ annihilates the difference of the two numerators. This step does
not cancel $L$ in the unlocalized ring.

Let $\bm{\mathbb A}_{\RES}\in\RES[1]$ be the disjoint union of
$\G_m$ and the degree-one point. Its ungraded class is $L$.
Its lift is $\mathcal O^\times\sqcup(1+\mathcal M)$, which is definably
bijective to the closed unit ball $\mathcal O$, by translating the second
summand to $\mathcal M$. Consequently
\[
 \bb E_g([\bm{\mathbb A}_{\RES}])=L^{-1}L=1.
\]
Replace the two representatives by
\[
 \widehat{\bm U}_b=\bm U_b\times\bm{\mathbb A}_{\RES}^{\,N-k},
 \qquad
 \widehat{\bm U}'_b=\bm U'_b\times\bm{\mathbb A}_{\RES}^{\,N-k'}.
\]
Both have degree $N$, and \eqref{geometric:fubini:clearing} says that their $\widetilde{\bb E}_g$-values
are equal. Their VF lifts are definably bijective to
$A_b\times\mathcal O^{N-k}$ and $A'_b\times\mathcal O^{N-k'}$.

The integer $N$ is part of the finite witness package. More precisely,
express \eqref{geometric:fubini:clearing} by the finite stabilized RES-bijection criterion
used in Theorem~\ref{fubini}, and include the padding exponents, all its objects,
bounds and graphs, and both positive lifting bijections. The validity of
each fixed package is first order over the original ground. These
conditions cover every ambient parameter $b$. Compactness supplies
finitely many packages; a finite definable partition fixes $k,k',N$ and
the witness formulas on each part. No uniform bound on the
denominator-clearing exponents is assumed before this argument.

Work on one such part. The relative RV calculation in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{fubini} applies to $\widetilde{\bb E}_g$ with $\chi_g$ in place of $\chi_b$.
For clarity, if $X$ is a definable RV set,
$J\subseteq X\times\Gamma^r$ is definable, and
\[
 Y=\{(x,z)\in X\times(\RV^\times)^r:\vrv(z)\in J_x\},
\]
then its unnormalized form is
\begin{equation}\label{geometric:fubini:push}
 \widetilde{\bb E}_g([Y])=g^r\sum_e e\,\widetilde{\bb E}_g([X_e]),\qquad
 g=[\G_m],\quad X_e=\{x:\chi_g(J_x)=e\}.
\end{equation}
This follows from the same monomial decomposition and Fubini formula
for $\chi_g$ on the $\Gamma$-sort. The sum is finite, and its level sets are definable over the
original ground. For the first-order Euler certificates, use finite
decompositions into relatively open rational polyhedra: a nonempty
cell of dimension $j$ has geometric Euler characteristic $(-1)^j$.
The cell formulas, dimensions and nonemptiness flags are included in
the finite package. The varying value-torsor identity also remains valid,
since $\chi_g$ of a singleton is $1$. Thus the actual total-bijection
relations and the value-fiber relations in the finite stabilized
witnesses are preserved by $\widetilde{\bb E}_g$. Their common stabilization is
cancelled additively, as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{fubini}.

Include all these formulas in the same preparation package. On a
prepared base piece, let $d$ be the number of nonzero VF base coordinates
and write $\tau=(s,t)$ as in that proof. The padded total RV objects have
structure maps
\[
 q(\tau,u)=(s,p_\tau(u)),\qquad
 q'(\tau,u')=(s,p'_\tau(u')),
\]
and both belong to $\RV[d+N]$. Finiteness of these maps follows from the
actual RV fibers of the total VF lifts, as before. In particular, the
retained coordinates $t$ receive no additional VF lifts. The relative
calculation and complete stabilized witnesses give equality of the
$\widetilde{\bb E}_g$-values of the two total objects. Multiplication by the common
factor $L^{-(d+N)}$ and \eqref{geometric:fubini:normalization} give equality of their
geometric values.

On the chosen part the padded total VF spaces are
$A|_{B_i}\times\mathcal O^{N-k}$ and $A'|_{B_i}\times\mathcal O^{N-k'}$.
Since $\bb E_g(\int[\mathcal O])=1$, their geometric values agree with those of
$A|_{B_i}$ and $A'|_{B_i}$, respectively. Finite additivity over the base
partition proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{rem}
Taking $A'=\varnothing$ gives the corresponding vanishing statement.
The proposition is stated in the ungraded localized target
$R_g(\mathbb S)$. It uses no injectivity of the natural homomorphism
from the homogeneous degree-zero affine localization to that target.
\end{rem}
